## Dossier part: z4c (run contract-z4c-2026-11-02, round 1 accepted)

# z4c — addresses for action-local region support

Proposal for council review; not a spec edit or an implementation claim.
Bead: `b-isa-spec-z4c`; blocks reference slice `b-isa-spec-fuk.3`.

## Defect and scope

ISA 0.9 §4.6, `qregion`, requires unconditional function-entry allocation
and image preparation, function scope, and function-exit restoration and
release. §16.2 permits a declaration inside either qif arm and inside a
qloop, including a zero-trip loop. §11 Matching requires every generated
leaf to end at a point whose iteration vector has the point's lexical loop
depth. K64 supplies non-row addresses only in a containing **oracle**.
K65 explicitly excludes implicit region restoration from cleanup carriers.

Consequently the following admitted action has mandatory leaves without
legal terminal addresses:

```text
action @gap (t:wire mut) () () () ()
regs {  } bounds {0}
^entry():
qloop 0 {
qregion local 0 1
}
x t
```

The associated signature has `mut:[1]`, `bounds:[0]`. The declaration is
`entry:1`; there is no executed iteration. `iteration:[]` has the wrong
depth and `[0]` is outside a zero-trip loop. Wildcard selectors can match
existing coordinates but cannot create a coordinate. Omitting the leaves
violates §4.6 even for an entirely default schedule. Calling them oracle
support contradicts K64's validation domain.

This is an S0 contract defect under rubric C5/C8: implementers must invent
an address or reject valid source. North Star §4.2 requires structured
coherent regions with unconditional workspace support; §4.4 requires an
independently implementable scheduler contract. Forbidding nested region
declarations would narrow the existing ISA. The fix preserves every
program, including the full SQLite/demo scope; no placement restriction is
proposed.

## Alternatives and worked witness

| Rule | Result on the witness |
|---|---|
| Action-root support pseudo-points (recommended) | Allocate local once at `$construct/[]/0`; execute X once at `entry:2/[]/0`; release local once at `$release/[]/0`. |
| Give declarations a separate hoisted coordinate type | Sound if it explicitly separates declaration coordinates from body iterations; requires another selector encoding and validation rule. |
| Use the lexical point with an invented iteration | `[]` violates depth; `[0]` names a nonexistent iteration. Requires a different normative rule, not an implementation convention. |
| Omit support or reject this source | Violates §4.6; rejected. |

Hand execution: for t=0, forward produces t=1; for t=1, t=0. The local
wire is allocated at zero and released at zero in either case. The inverse
restores t exactly and also allocates/releases the local wire once. The
loop body executes zero times. Resource counts before native decomposition
are one allocation, one X, one release. This is a derivation from the
clauses, not a newly run interpreter test. The bead reports a reference
test and diagnostics; those runs were not repeated in this job.

## Recommended exact spec diff

Insert after §11 **Non-row addresses (K64)**, before Whole-cleanup selection:

```diff
+**Action-local region support (K-new).** For each action or perm_action
+invocation, collect all function-local qregion declarations, including
+declarations nested in zero-trip qloops and in either qif arm. Each
+declaration creates one region per invocation, not one per loop visit.
+Traverse declarations in canonical function text order, recursively
+visiting the then arm before the else arm and a loop body once, for
+function-entry allocation and image preparation. Their terminal point is
+$construct in that action. Function-exit image restoration and release
+traverse the declarations in reverse order and have terminal point
+$release. Within a declaration, use the bound library's region creation
+expansion; restoration/release is its exact inverse. These support leaves
+are unconditional as specified in §3.5 and §4.6.
+Number leaves contiguously from zero within each of these pseudo-points
+in forward instantiated support order, including allocation, image gates
+and release leaves, excluding the action body and semantic callee
+subtrees. Each leaf retains its allocation identity. Their own instance
+and source-qloop iteration vectors are []; outer invocation edges retain
+their actual coordinates. At that action level the support has no step
+or phase and matches only wildcard step and phase, under §11 Matching.
+A selector ending at an enclosing oracle level retains that level's
+existing step/phase interpretation.
+Raw inversion of an already expanded trace retains forward pseudo-points,
+ordinals, invocation edges and allocation identities while adjointing
+operations. Source-adjoint formation continues to follow K62.
+Path validation admits $construct and $release for action and perm_action
+support. $step and $commit remain oracle-only. A reserved point with no
+leaves has an empty generated domain. K64's oracle partition and K65's
+exclusion of implicit region cleanup are unchanged.
```

Add to §0.2, assigning the next free K number at application time:

```text
K-new | §4.6, §11, §15 | ACTION-REGION-SUPPORT: action-local qregion
construction/release have reserved root support addresses, including
zero-trip declarations; no leaf changes. | b-isa-spec-z4c | S | –
```

The traversal rule resolves several declarations and both arms without
mistaking source-loop visits for declarations. It adds addresses, not
cleanup realisations or source instructions.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No change to canonical object text, semantic envelopes, `ref-0.9.json`, its
hash or generated leaf order is intended. Unaffected object closure hashes
stay fixed; the bead's witness closure hash is reported evidence, not
recomputed here. Address-bearing schedules/certificates for affected
actions must use the new coordinates, so their own serialized hashes
change. Published 0.8 behavior is untouched. Reference, VM, scheduler and
checker must implement identical action support partitioning; clause
extraction/coverage must include the new paragraphs. A catalog change is
unnecessary if its existing region construction already meets §4.6.

## Conformance vectors and verification

After acceptance, add these vectors to suite F and schedule suite H:

1. The zero-trip witness above: both t basis values, forward/inverse;
   exactly the three leaves and coordinates given above; no loop-body leaf.
2. Two declarations, one inside each qif arm: both constructed once in
   then/else text order regardless of selector value, released in reverse
   order. Nested zero-trip loops add no iteration coordinate to support.
3. `qregion r 0 8 @image` with one-byte image `01`: construction emits its
   allocation then X on bit 0; release undoes image then allocation. Check
   contiguous per-point ordinals and raw inverse identity retention.
4. A caller loop invokes this action twice: callee support has `[]`, caller
   edge has `[0]` or `[1]`; no alias of allocation identities across calls.
5. Concrete action `$step`/`$commit` reject; action support with nonempty
   own iteration/instance does not match; duplicate maximal matching
   entries on an actual support leaf reject by K55. Empty support domains
   cannot witness a tie.

These are proposed vectors, not claims of a passing implementation.
The exact commands used for the derivation were `sed -n '954,1617p'
spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md` and `sed -n '2401,2842p'
spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md`, plus inspection of the supplied bead.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K-entry repair**, ahead of the blocked reference slice.
Waiting for 0.10 would leave an existing legal 0.9 source without an
address. Council assigns the K number, applies the text, publishes the
vectors, regenerates the 0.8-to-0.9 diff and clause indexes, and authorizes
the reference owner to finish slice .3. The bead stays open until that
contract/implementation acceptance work is complete.

## Dossier part: adg (run contract-adg-2026-11-02, round 1 accepted)

# adg — loaded-program memory lifetime across launches

Proposal for council review; `b-isa-spec-adg`.

## Defect and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §7.1 defines initial memory, allocation table and bump pointer
without saying whether initialization occurs at load or every run.
§3.2 places the register ledger at program scope; §10 gives a run
exclusive ownership only until CLOSED; §7.4 and T4 restore a run's entry
state on eligible reversal. These strongly favor program-lifetime state
but do not explicitly settle classical memory initialization.

Dialect 0.3 draft §§6.5–6.6 uses a persistent qreg table in device memory
and requires parameter/result roots to survive entry calls. North Star
§3.1's register handles and §5's REPL/library device model require
cross-entry quantum state. A fresh-every-run interpretation loses the
table while live registers remain. This is a contract ambiguity with
observable results, S0 under C1/C5; persistence is the compatible reading.
It adds no restriction to the North Star, including repeated SQLite or
kernel invocations.

## Alternatives and hand-worked witness

Layout: ptr_width=16, H=16, A_tab=2, S_cells=0, null_size=1,
heap_base=16, heap_limit_max=16; a one-byte global g at base 1 with image
00. Run A allocates one byte (returns 16) and stores 7 into g, then
completes and is CLOSED without reversal. Run B loads g and allocates
one byte. Under persistence it returns `(7,17)`; under reinitialization
it returns `(0,16)`. These are distinct visible results from the same
loaded program. Freeing 16 does not reclaim storage under §7.1.

If A is instead successfully REVERSED before CLOSED, its allocation
and store are undone, and B returns `(0,16)`. Merely closing A does not
reverse it. An eligible failed run retains its executed prefix until it
is reversed; closing a failed run does not silently roll it back.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Program-lifetime memory and ledger (recommended) | Matches §10 ownership and the dialect's persistent table. |
| Reinitialize on each run | Would need a different host-held table and an explicit reset/lifetime ABI; it changes existing consumer assumptions. |
| Implicit reset when no register is live | Still changes classical allocation/global results; no clause authorizes it. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Insert after §7.1's Initial state sentence:

```diff
+That state is established once when the program is loaded. Classical
+memory, allocation-table entries and the bump pointer belong to the
+loaded program and persist across runs. For sequential launches, a new
+run starts from the state left by the preceding CLOSED run. Closing a run does not restore its
+entry state. If the preceding run was successfully REVERSED before
+being CLOSED, its reversible state is its restored entry state (§7.4,
+§10, T4). A FAILED run otherwise leaves its executed prefix in place.
+Register-ledger entries and live register identities likewise persist
+under §3.3; ownership ends at CLOSED, not the register lifetime. Unloading
+and loading a program creates a new program instance with initial state;
+register ids of the old instance are not imported into it.
```

Append to §10's ownership paragraph:

```diff
+Persistence does not relax exclusive ownership of touched registers or
+memory while a run is not CLOSED. Separate loaded program instances have
+separate state; initialization is never repeated merely to start a run.
```

All examples here use sequential CLOSED runs. This wording does not add a
blanket prohibition on disjoint concurrent runs or invent a memory merge
rule. The existing ownership restrictions still govern overlapping uses.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

This pins an existing reading: no source, envelope, target or catalog byte
changes and no hash re-pin. A consumer that reset memory on each run must
change its load/run lifecycle; plugin qreg-table code should not change.
Launch bindings and certificates that replay a later entry must include
its actual entry-state/preparation assumptions rather than presume a
fresh load. Domain claims do not acquire coverage of all histories merely
by showing one initialized run. Header diagnostics already include
E_INVALID_STATE; no new numeric status is proposed.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Test load→A→CLOSED→B and load→A→REVERSED→CLOSED→B with the results
above; check every byte, table live bit, bump pointer, register identity
and token lifetime. Add a failed A whose prefix stored 7: B sees 7 unless
that prefix was reversed. If B passes a register still owned by A before
A is CLOSED, it must return E_INVALID_STATE before effects. Fresh loading
restores g=0 and bump=16. A stored id can be recovered by qreg.from_id in
the next run after ownership is released. These are hand derivations;
the scalar state transitions are checked by
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md), not by a
new reference execution.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K wording repair** to §§7.1/10 with lifecycle vectors.
Council should explicitly accept program scope under existing ownership, then
VM/reference owners validate their lifecycle and the dialect updates its
cross-reference. Keep adg open until the contract decision lands.

## Dossier part: checker-diagnostics (run contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02, round 1 accepted)

# Checker question 2 — diagnostic-code ownership

Disposition proposal for council review; source request 2 in
`../bennett-conformance/checker/.tmp/CROSS_REPO_BEADS.md`.

## Defect classification and North Star scope

ISA §12 requires rejection of invalid certificates but defines no global
checker error-code registry. §11 separately mandates
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` and K55's multiple-maximal-entry diagnostic.
Launch §10 enumerates executor statuses; §5.4 enumerates program fault
outcomes. These are different interfaces, and the latter two lists do
not imply an unlisted E_CERT registry.

Two checkers rejecting the same invalid certificate under different
documented local codes compute the same specified verdict. The lack of
a registry is therefore **not an established contract defect**. It is a
possible diagnostics portability improvement (rubric C8/S1). North Star
§§4.4/5.1 requires independent checkability and honest labels, not a
particular spelling for every checker-local code. No capability is lost
by leaving those codes local. Inventing required codes would make
otherwise conformant checkers fail an unstated condition.

## Alternatives and concrete witness

Certificate A omits a table claim's required `expected`. Certificate B
has a complete table and invocation but repeats a component in its domain.
K13 requires A to reject; §12.2 K71 requires B to reject. Checker 1 may
use `E_CERT_RELATION` and `E_CERT_DOMAIN`; checker 2 may use
`missing_expected` and `duplicate_component`. All four diagnostic
spellings are illustrative local choices, not normative codes.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Keep codes implementation-defined (recommended) | Preserve existing contracts; test local documented codes and normative verdicts separately. |
| Add a small report-location convention | Useful optional interoperability, without reserving every local code. |
| Publish a full ISA-wide E_CERT registry | New interface/versioning work; no evidence it is required for correctness. |
| Reuse launch statuses or quantum fault codes | Conflates checker rejection with run failure or quantum oracle outcome. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

No semantic change or mandatory rejection registry is required. If the
council wants explicit wording, append to §12.3:

```diff
+Checker diagnostic codes other than codes explicitly mandated elsewhere
+in this specification are implementation-defined. A checker SHOULD
+document its stable code meanings and report the failed normative
+obligation and certificate location with a rejection. Such codes are
+scoped by checker name and version; their spelling does not alter the
+verdict or any executor status/fault code. This convention does not
+replace mandated E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE behavior or K55 diagnostics.
```

The original request's mandatory report schema would be a new API; it
should not be inferred from present text. An application needing that
API can version its checker-report format independently.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No source, object, target, catalog, schedule or certificate bytes need
change. Optional checker report fields affect only that report's format
and identity. Consumers must not compare an implementation's code against
an invented normative registry. A normative schedule failure still needs
its mandated code and validation stage.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use A/B above as negative schema/domain tests; assert rejection and the
normative clause, and separately assert the implementation's documented
local code. For an otherwise valid schedule containing duplicate maximal
matching entries, retain K55's named diagnostic and staged rejection.
Do not turn a checker-local mismatch into a program fault or invalid
source verdict. This is a clause derivation; no checker test was run here.
The common document verification checks this disposition's required
sections and source links.

## Release and after acceptance

**No new K requirement and no 0.10 blocker.** Optional 0.9 editorial
clarification is sufficient. The orchestrator can disposition request 2
as documentation/API guidance; it should not file an ISA semantic bug
solely for missing E_CERT spellings. Existing checker tests may continue
to pin their own stable documented codes.

## Dossier part: 5u3 (run contract-5u3-2026-11-02, round 1 accepted)

# 5u3 — exact pointer fixpoints without verdict-changing caps

Proposal for the dialect council; `b-dialect-spec-5u3`, linked to
`bennett-plugin` bp-fj1. This job writes no dialect or plugin source.

## Defect classification and North Star scope

Both dialect `Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` (draft 0.2.12) and the 0.3 draft
§5 require every verdict/candidate/dispatch domain to come from the
specified reference analysis. §5.1 caps INT atoms at K=4096, but does not
cap explicit object offsets or PT6–PT8 memory intervals. PT2/PT5 use
finite-width modular pointer arithmetic. Hence an explicit enumeration
is finite but can have 2^61 members. §5.1 already admits residue classes
and `*`; it does not require enumerative storage.

The bead establishes a severe implementation/performance problem, **not
by itself an inconsistent or undefined reference semantics**. A sound
over-approximation may still change a compile-time verdict, which §5
forbids. The proposed thresholds (4096 offsets, 4 outside extent, 32 for
unknown extent; max(64,extent/4) slots) are not justified as exact. They
must not become normative merely because one plugin uses them.

North Star §§0/3.3/6.1 require arbitrary defined bounded programs,
whole-program analysis and substitutable compilers; §8 rung 4 requires
unmodified SQLite. Widening that introduces a false handle-use/escape
rejection narrows those capabilities. Removing SQLite features, changing
its source, suppressing vectorisation or declaring valid pointer patterns
unsupported does not resolve the issue. Keep bp-fj1 open for practical
convergence; do not declare the rung delivered from a coarse fixpoint.

## Plugin evidence actually inspected

`../bennett-plugin-rung4/.tmp/rung4/PROGRESS.md` reports 177381 slots
plateauing by round 7 on the N=16, LLVM-18 closure, with additional atoms
still growing. The tail of `.tmp/rung4/prof/trace-k.log` shows round 39,
177381 slots, growth in SQLite integer/value sets, then
`§5.2 PT8/PT9/PT12 budget exhausted` at a memcpy in
`@sqlite3CreateForeignKey`, exit 1 after 3401.31 seconds. It does **not**
demonstrate a converged exact reference fixpoint or prove that remaining
growth is only a pointer chain. The supplied earlier 800k-slot report is
historical bead evidence, not reproduced here. No full sibling gate was
run, and no plugin implementation was copied.

## Alternatives and worked recurrence

Take width W=64 and an unfiltered inclusion cycle with one provenance X:

```text
S initially contains offset 0
S includes { (o+8) mod 2^64 : o in S }
```

Its exact least solution is `{o: o=0 mod 8, 0<=o<2^64}`. There are
`2^64/gcd(8,2^64)=2^61` offsets, representable by one residue class.
For W=8 the set is exactly 0,8,...,248 (32 offsets), followed by 0.
Starting at 3 instead gives 3,11,...,251; adding a seed at 4 gives two
cosets, not `*`. Extent=16 does not permit dropping offsets ≥16:
PT2 retains original provenance and adds EXT/top for possibly invalid
addresses; it does not turn numerical out-of-range into host escape.

A full-width same-object memory copy from offset o to o+8 can similarly
propagate one stored atom along that relation. An exact periodic memory
summary must retain storage width, intervals and value/root provenance;
merging all fields into one slot may turn a nearby integer field into a
handle-derived value. That would be a different verdict even though it
is conservative for aliasing.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Unbounded explicit enumeration | Same reference semantics, often impractical. Not required by the existing text. |
| Normative widening with the bead's thresholds | Deterministic only with further rules for ordering/stickiness/joins; can reject previously admitted programs. Not acceptable without a capability-preserving lowering for every newly coarse case. |
| Exact symbolic sets and translation-cycle closure (recommended) | Preserves verdicts and handles this witness compactly. Does not claim every SQLite recurrence has this form. |
| Resource/time cap with an explicit unfinished build | Honest operational outcome, not a completed analysis or a source rejection. Remains implementation work. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Add a dialect K/clarification adjacent to §5.1's K and §5.2's least
fixpoint, in both maintained maps:

```text
The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.
```

Add to §24:

```text
Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.
```

This recommends an exact acceleration and explicitly **declines new
verdict-affecting bounds**. If council instead wants a new abstract
domain, it must supply canonical joins, evaluation order independence,
ROOT/partial-access rules and a lowering that preserves all programs
previously in scope; the current bead does not establish those properties.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

An exact representation clarification changes no materialised program,
ISA object/certificate or catalog bytes. A dialect publication still
gets its required revision/mapping identity. A threshold widening could
change descriptors, dispatch domains, diagnostics and emitted object
hashes, so cannot masquerade as a local plugin optimization. Plugin and
independent dialect checker should compare normalized exact results on
small-width cases, not implementation data-structure counts.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Enumerate all W=8, stride-8 cases above and compare their symbolic cosets;
W=64 is a symbolic cardinality test, never a 2^61-element allocation.
Test stride 0, stride 1, multiple seeds, a cycle with a filter (must not
use the pure-cycle rule), and GEP with I(a)<P(a). Memory vectors include
same-width full copies, overlapping partial copies and adjacent handle/
integer fields; retain PT6's non-handle top and ROOT distinctions. Check
identical verdicts independent of worklist order. Mathematical cosets are
checked by [contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md).
The SQLite exact fixpoint and production gate remain unverified here.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **dialect K clarification on the current 0.2/0.3 tracks**,
not an ISA-0.10 dependency. As stated, 5u3 is primarily an implementation
scalability request. After acceptance, add the finite vectors and let the
plugin owner establish exact convergence on both LLVM majors and the
full rung-4 closure. No new source restriction is authorized by this note.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:991:- `ROOT(r)`: a handle with root r (the allocation site of a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call, §6.5);

../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1005:### 5.2 Rules
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1007:Before the fixpoint, seed the initialiser atoms of every global, mutable or constant,
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1013:The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV. "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1020:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1021:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1022:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1023:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1024:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1025:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1026:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1027:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1028:| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1029:| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1030:| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1031:| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1038:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.

../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6795:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6885:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.

../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:8182:| `addrspacecast` | materialise the incoming target's specified conversion between address-space representations, including any non-width conversion; `lift.mov` only for bit-preserving equal-width conversions. For integral zero-extend/truncate conversions emit `lift.zext`/`lift.trunc` to P(destination) |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:8183:| `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | Let P=P(a), I=I(a) for p's address space. Arithmetic on the low I bits is modulo 2^I; only representation bits above I are preserved, also for constant expressions. Canonical rows: acc is the pointer operand (when I<P, first `t = lift.trunc.i<I> P_operand` and acc is t). For each non-constant sequential index in index order: sign-extend/truncate to I (omit the cast when already I wide); if its element allocation size s≠1 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> (s mod 2^I)` then `t = lift.mul index c`, else use the index directly; emit `acc′ = lift.add acc t`. Constant sequential indices, interpreted signed at their source width then scaled by s, and constant structure field offsets accumulate into Δ mod 2^I, with no per-index rows. After the dynamic sequence, if Δ≠0 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> Δ` then `acc′ = lift.add acc c`. For I=P the last acc′ row writes v; with no dynamic index and Δ=0 the only row is `v = lift.mov P_operand`. For I<P always emit recombination, even when acc equals trunc(P_operand), in this order: `lo = lift.zext.i<P> acc`; `c = lift.const.i<P> <ones above I>`; `hi = lift.and P_operand c`; `v = lift.or hi lo`. Every emitted row is charged normally |

../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:970:### 5.1 Abstract objects and atoms
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:983:- the pseudo-object `EXT` (memory of unknown provenance: host memory reached through a pointer
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:995:- `ROOT(r)`: a handle with root r (the allocation site of a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call, §6.5);
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1000:or address". ⊤ never stands for a `ROOT` atom (handle provenance is always explicit); for a
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1009:### 5.2 Rules
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1011:Before the fixpoint, seed the initialiser atoms of every global, mutable or constant,
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1017:The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV. "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1024:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1025:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1026:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1027:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1028:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1029:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1030:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1031:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1032:| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1033:| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1034:| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1035:| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1042:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1112:**PT12 spelling witnesses (non-normative).** Explicit address spaces, volatile/weak qualifiers, syncscope and alignment retain the same N9 value effects and native materialised outcomes; the pointer operand keeps its address-space type in the generated load and store.
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1160:PT12 checked ROOT propagation: `make` returns the fresh qalloc ROOT to its caller;
../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1182:same derivation. The PT8 companion copies the stack image to a mutable global

../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6893:**PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.

../bennett-dialect-spec/spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:8191:| `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | Let P=P(a), I=I(a) for p's address space. Arithmetic on the low I bits is modulo 2^I; only representation bits above I are preserved, also for constant expressions. Canonical rows: acc is the pointer operand (when I<P, first `t = lift.trunc.i<I> P_operand` and acc is t). For each non-constant sequential index in index order: sign-extend/truncate to I (omit the cast when already I wide); if its element allocation size s≠1 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> (s mod 2^I)` then `t = lift.mul index c`, else use the index directly; emit `acc′ = lift.add acc t`. Constant sequential indices, interpreted signed at their source width then scaled by s, and constant structure field offsets accumulate into Δ mod 2^I, with no per-index rows. After the dynamic sequence, if Δ≠0 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> Δ` then `acc′ = lift.add acc c`. For I=P the last acc′ row writes v; with no dynamic index and Δ=0 the only row is `v = lift.mov P_operand`. For I<P always emit recombination, even when acc equals trunc(P_operand), in this order: `lo = lift.zext.i<P> acc`; `c = lift.const.i<P> <ones above I>`; `hi = lift.and P_operand c`; `v = lift.or hi lo`. Every emitted row is charged normally |

880:### 4.2 Lifted rows (oracle bodies and lprocs)

969:### 4.4 Gates
987:### 4.5 Copy, XOR, exchange, in-place
995:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..)`; written `qinplace @g @ginv tk x` when all three lists are empty (and only then) | **Well-formed** iff `@g` and `@ginv` are oracles whose `in` slots are x's width n followed by the widths of `ins` in order, whose `tgt` width is n, whose fault widths are equal (F), whose `mem` slots match the `mem` regions (role `in`, §3.3's matching rule) and whose `args` types are those of `args`; x, the `ins` wires and the effective control context are pairwise disjoint. **Sem**: `t = qalloc any n; f = qalloc any F; oracle_call @g tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qexch x t; oracle_call @ginv tk (x, ins..) t f (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

../bennett/docs/council/contract-z4c-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:339:## 3. The dialect: what an out-of-tree plugin can rely on
../bennett/docs/council/contract-z4c-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:345:### 3.1 Mechanisms
../bennett/docs/council/contract-z4c-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:353:| Taint: classical vs reversible vs coherent | **Analysis, not types**: a context-sensitive interprocedural analysis over data dependence, control dependence and memory effects, from marker results and region entries, over the whole-program call graph, with polyvariant specialisation when one function is used both classically and coherently. | No per-value attribute survives optimisation and none is needed. "Untainted" is defined relative to the resolved call graph, never per translation unit. |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-z4c-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:354:| Control as structure: adjoint, controlled-by-qubit, compute-with-uncompute, reversible-tier entry | **Function granularity**: a region is outlined to a function (LLVM's `CodeExtractor`) and entered through a marker call `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, budget, args…)`, `__bennett_adj(@f, …)`, `__bennett_compute(@f, …)`, or `__bennett_reversible(@f, budget, …)` for the reversible tier. The plugin generates controlled and adjoint bodies on demand from one source body. **Well-formedness:** a `ctrl`/`adj`/`compute` region is unitary: user measurement inside it is refused (or, when the front end asks for it, deferred by the deferred-measurement principle, which the certificate states); allocations inside are released clean or borrowed and restored; the compiler's own measurement-based uncomputation is the sole exception, and until its controlled form is fixed in the language's certificate kinds (§9.6) **unitary uncomputation is the fallback under control**. Exceptions and unwinding (`invoke`/`landingpad`, Rust panics, C++ `throw`) inside a region are step-machine control flow where the handler is in the region, and a fault code where it is not. | Front ends that want `when`-style syntax do the outlining themselves (Julia macro, clang attribute, Rust proc-macro) and emit the marker call; the dialect does not require it. Generating a body needs the callee's IR, which is why the plugin runs at link time (§3.3). |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-z4c-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:358:| Effects | Marker calls on the device side for the effects the device models (console, clock, entropy, block device); everything else is a host call. | Effect semantics per tier are in §4.2; an unvirtualisable effect inside a coherent region is a fault code on the executions that reach it, and a coverage-report line at compile time. |

Devices the program touches are virtualised as reversible state inside the device (an in-memory VFS, a console register, a device model) and become part of the oracle's state. Classical inputs are fixed before entry; outputs leave through measurement after exit. An effect the device cannot virtualise is a fault code on the executions that reach it. | refined by the materialised normalisation of §3.2; immediate UB is a fault code |

../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:342:## 3. The dialect: what an out-of-tree plugin can rely on
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:348:### 3.1 Mechanisms
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:356:| Taint: classical vs reversible vs coherent | **Analysis, not types**: a context-sensitive interprocedural analysis over data dependence, control dependence and memory effects, from marker results and region entries, over the whole-program call graph, with polyvariant specialisation when one function is used both classically and coherently. | No per-value attribute survives optimisation and none is needed. "Untainted" is defined relative to the resolved call graph, never per translation unit. |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:357:| Control as structure: adjoint, controlled-by-qubit, compute-with-uncompute, reversible-tier entry | **Function granularity**: a region is outlined to a function (LLVM's `CodeExtractor`) and entered through a marker call `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, budget, args…)`, `__bennett_adj(@f, …)`, `__bennett_compute(@f, …)`, or `__bennett_reversible(@f, budget, …)` for the reversible tier. The plugin generates controlled and adjoint bodies on demand from one source body. **Well-formedness:** a `ctrl`/`adj`/`compute` region is unitary: user measurement inside it is refused (or, when the front end asks for it, deferred by the deferred-measurement principle, which the certificate states); allocations inside are released clean or borrowed and restored; the compiler's own measurement-based uncomputation is the sole exception, and until its controlled form is fixed in the language's certificate kinds (§9.6) **unitary uncomputation is the fallback under control**. Exceptions and unwinding (`invoke`/`landingpad`, Rust panics, C++ `throw`) inside a region are step-machine control flow where the handler is in the region, and a fault code where it is not. | Front ends that want `when`-style syntax do the outlining themselves (Julia macro, clang attribute, Rust proc-macro) and emit the marker call; the dialect does not require it. Generating a body needs the callee's IR, which is why the plugin runs at link time (§3.3). |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:361:| Effects | Marker calls on the device side for the effects the device models (console, clock, entropy, block device); everything else is a host call. | Effect semantics per tier are in §4.2; an unvirtualisable effect inside a coherent region is a fault code on the executions that reach it, and a coverage-report line at compile time. |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:363:### 3.2 Pipeline placement and the cost of staying in-tree
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:426:    which absolute *native stack* address bits still influence the
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:475:  native code generation of fat objects, must preserve the dynamic

../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:682:| reversible | device | recorded on the effect tape forwards, replayed backwards; the device models console, clock, entropy, block storage as reversible device models; entered through `__bennett_reversible` or a driver mode | refined by the materialised normalisation of §3.2; immediate UB is a fault, recorded |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-5u3-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:683:| coherent | device | **closed and effect-free**: no external effect may occur inside a coherent region. Devices the program touches are virtualised as reversible state inside the device (an in-memory VFS, a console register, a device model) and become part of the oracle's state. Classical inputs are fixed before entry; outputs leave through measurement after exit. An effect the device cannot virtualise is a fault code on the executions that reach it. | refined by the materialised normalisation of §3.2; immediate UB is a fault code |

../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:308:## 2. The architecture, in one picture
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:315:  Link time      bennett-driver: whole-program bitcode (LTO) ─► LLVM's own optimiser (unmodified)
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:318:                   taint over the resolved call graph → region outlining → controlled/adjoint
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:321:  Objects        fat object: native host code + ISA object(s) + certificate (ISA spec, §4.4)
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:339:## 3. The dialect: what an out-of-tree plugin can rely on
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:345:### 3.1 Mechanisms
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:353:| Taint: classical vs reversible vs coherent | **Analysis, not types**: a context-sensitive interprocedural analysis over data dependence, control dependence and memory effects, from marker results and region entries, over the whole-program call graph, with polyvariant specialisation when one function is used both classically and coherently. | No per-value attribute survives optimisation and none is needed. "Untainted" is defined relative to the resolved call graph, never per translation unit. |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:354:| Control as structure: adjoint, controlled-by-qubit, compute-with-uncompute, reversible-tier entry | **Function granularity**: a region is outlined to a function (LLVM's `CodeExtractor`) and entered through a marker call `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, budget, args…)`, `__bennett_adj(@f, …)`, `__bennett_compute(@f, …)`, or `__bennett_reversible(@f, budget, …)` for the reversible tier. The plugin generates controlled and adjoint bodies on demand from one source body. **Well-formedness:** a `ctrl`/`adj`/`compute` region is unitary: user measurement inside it is refused (or, when the front end asks for it, deferred by the deferred-measurement principle, which the certificate states); allocations inside are released clean or borrowed and restored; the compiler's own measurement-based uncomputation is the sole exception, and until its controlled form is fixed in the language's certificate kinds (§9.6) **unitary uncomputation is the fallback under control**. Exceptions and unwinding (`invoke`/`landingpad`, Rust panics, C++ `throw`) inside a region are step-machine control flow where the handler is in the region, and a fault code where it is not. | Front ends that want `when`-style syntax do the outlining themselves (Julia macro, clang attribute, Rust proc-macro) and emit the marker call; the dialect does not require it. Generating a body needs the callee's IR, which is why the plugin runs at link time (§3.3). |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:358:| Effects | Marker calls on the device side for the effects the device models (console, clock, entropy, block device); everything else is a host call. | Effect semantics per tier are in §4.2; an unvirtualisable effect inside a coherent region is a fault code on the executions that reach it, and a coverage-report line at compile time. |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:360:### 3.2 Pipeline placement and the cost of staying in-tree
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:423:    which absolute *native stack* address bits still influence the
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:472:  native code generation of fat objects, must preserve the dynamic
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:477:### 3.3 Compilation model: link time, whole program, fat objects
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:489:  resolves taint over the whole call graph, and produces a **fat object**:
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:513:## 4. The virtual ISA and the device
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:515:### 4.1 Why the VM is the revolution
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:551:### 4.2 Execution semantics by tier
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:679:| reversible | device | recorded on the effect tape forwards, replayed backwards; the device models console, clock, entropy, block storage as reversible device models; entered through `__bennett_reversible` or a driver mode | refined by the materialised normalisation of §3.2; immediate UB is a fault, recorded |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:680:| coherent | device | **closed and effect-free**: no external effect may occur inside a coherent region. Devices the program touches are virtualised as reversible state inside the device (an in-memory VFS, a console register, a device model) and become part of the oracle's state. Classical inputs are fixed before entry; outputs leave through measurement after exit. An effect the device cannot virtualise is a fault code on the executions that reach it. | refined by the materialised normalisation of §3.2; immediate UB is a fault code |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-checker-diagnostics-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:698:### 4.3 Resources

../bennett/docs/council/contract-adg-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:551:### 4.2 Execution semantics by tier
../bennett/docs/council/contract-adg-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:679:| reversible | device | recorded on the effect tape forwards, replayed backwards; the device models console, clock, entropy, block storage as reversible device models; entered through `__bennett_reversible` or a driver mode | refined by the materialised normalisation of §3.2; immediate UB is a fault, recorded |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-adg-2026-11-02/round-1/prompt.md:680:| coherent | device | **closed and effect-free**: no external effect may occur inside a coherent region. Devices the program touches are virtualised as reversible state inside the device (an in-memory VFS, a console register, a device model) and become part of the oracle's state. Classical inputs are fixed before entry; outputs leave through measurement after exit. An effect the device cannot virtualise is a fault code on the executions that reach it. | refined by the materialised normalisation of §3.2; immediate UB is a fault code |
